## lf-gamma-creation-20261029-r1 (launched 01:43, landed 01:58, triaged 01:58–02:01)

The review covered §§39.6, 39.7 and 40, with §§39.1–39.4 as the caller. It does not cover §§41–43, which were written after the packet was built. The packet was built before the C₁ double count in (G.240)–(G.241) was removed, but the review's arithmetic check (S24) is consistent with the corrected form.

Verdict: no fatal finding, three serious findings, eight minor ones. Parts (i) and (iii) are proved at the stated scope, conditional on R-m, (R-m′), R-fr(a), O6 and Bałaban's re-expansion. Part (ii) is correct but conditional on the unreviewed (G.229)–(G.231).

### Serious findings

1. **"R-fr is not used" is false.** Accepted. R-fr(a), the support reading r=δ_k/g_k, is load-bearing for the tube, for ρ_k^{tube}, and for e^{−c_br²} with c_br²≥2 log 2. Repaired in the last paragraph of §40.2 and in §40.5's premise list. Only p.277's amplitude O(ε_k) is avoided.
2. **(G.229)–(G.231) were missing from the open list.** Accepted. Added as §40.5 item 1. Part (ii) is marked as conditional on them.
3. **The interior bracket channels Y⊂Λ_{k+1} were omitted.** Accepted and repaired by a new bullet in §40.3. The Ω_{k+1} margin α_{1,k}−α_{1,k+1}/L≥α_{1,k}/2≥(β/2)α_{1,k} (map l.700) admits the same r_{α,k}, so the same ρ_k bound holds. This bullet has not been re-reviewed.

### Minor findings

- **O6 §5 misquoted** in the §40 preamble and in §40.4. Repaired. O6 §5 already bounds the brackets by a Schwarz coefficient of order ε_k/α_{1,k}, and "(1.36) with ε₁→ε_k′" is its 𝐕_k″ bound. (G.238)–(G.239) is now described as a re-derivation, not a correction.
- **Current-step terms.** Repaired using CRE (3.5) on p.265, which was read. Ω_{k+1} lies outside Z_k′^{∼8}, so the decay is superpolynomial. R_{k+1}≥(log g^{-2})^r follows from CRE p.245 and p.255.
- **Thresholds.** Repaired. The thresholds are now D2.4(10)(d)–(e), and (G.241) was recomputed with e^{max(5,3λ_L)κ} and without (L+2)⁴O(1). The order of the constant choices is open item 5. As a consequence, §41.2 now records that the operative resummation is D2.4(10)(b), with amplitude ϑC₃^{(10)}. The Kotecký–Preiss check with those constants is open (§42.5 item 5).
- **Bond-set separation in (G.233).** Repaired by citing Bałaban's (3.23)/(3.25). The layer count is recorded as not done.
- **Composite-walk payment in (G.235a).** Recorded as §40.5 item 6.
- **The p.277 attribution.** Repaired; the L^{n−k} phase is now labelled as a reading.
- **"O(ε_k) on the support" for RG1/RG2.** Repaired.
- **|Y₀| counts bonds, not cubes.** Repaired in §39.7.

## lf-gamma-vac-20261029-r1 (landed before 02:42, triaged 02:42–02:52)

Verdict: no fatal finding, three serious findings, seven minor ones. Parts (a) and (c) hold given (G.229), (G.230), (G.236) and (G.237). Part (b) holds as a transfer of Lemma 3's proof with R-L3. All of the reviewer's map citations were checked: R-L3 at l.1255, (2)(a), and D2.4(10)(b) at l.1456–1457.

### Serious findings

1. **The root bound in §41.3 is false for extended marks.** Accepted. The plain-expectation term has no decay in the extent of supp F, as D2.4(10)(b) states. It is replaced by:
   - (G.244a), D2.4(10)(b)'s relative-decay form with ‖F‖_tube, a proof transfer;
   - the sharper attached-cluster bound (G.244), 9K#(Z₀), from the first step of FV Lemma 5.6's proof;
   - a new count (G.244b), #(Z₀)≤81#(D′)+243d_{k+1}(Z₀;D′), derived by an Euler-walk covering;
   - a root count at fixed D′, derived by a depth-first-walk animal bound, 6400^{n−1};
   - the ratio bound (G.244c), ‖F‖_tube e^{(81A+729K+681)#(D′)}e^{(5+3λ_L)κ(m−1)}, which is volume-free but exponential in #(D′).
2. **The wrong operative vacuum bound in the caveat.** Accepted. The caveat is removed. The operative vacuum resummation is R-L3, and D2.4(10)(b) is the root bound. (G.243) now has O₃, and R-L3's conditions are listed: 18κ, ½(κ₁−1)≥9Lκ, the re-read p.20 condition, and the κ bounds.
3. **The status of (b).** Accepted. §41.2 now states that it is a transfer of Lemma 3's proof, conditional on (G.229)/(G.230) and the readings through (G.237). §41.4 lists all premises, including FV Lemma 5.6 and Theorem 5.8 and the (G.241) order at q₁=p₀.

### Minor findings

- The a-dependence through T_Y(a,·) and CRE's direct A_k-dependence: repaired in §41.1.
- RG2 p.8's size inequality is false: the citation is replaced by D2.4(2)(a), with #(Z*).
- (G.244)'s lossy a(Z₀): the sharper form is now the stated one.
- (κ₁−1)/2≥9Lκ misattribution: it is now R-L3's condition.
- FV Theorem 5.8 is for one variable: the several-variable step is stated.
- α_{1,k+1}≤(L/2)α_{1,k}: stated in the §40.3 bullet and in §41.4.
- The KP weight versus D2.4(2)'s weight at downstream callers: recorded in §41.4 as not checked.

## §44, the minimiser problem (written 02:55–03:21)

User: "Attempt to find a solution to the minimiser problem", "Continue", "Resume the minimiser problem". The problem is point (3) of (LF-A): the entries c(t) of the joint line are not local minimisers of their own data for t ≠ 0.

The route: RG I p.275 l.1–7 defines the near-domain terms on the class through the block data M^·(𝐔) on □₀ (reading R-data, which the LF note's bullet at l.527 already cites). By the feasible-set argument the composite is the layer's local minimiser P_nc(t), a member of 𝒮_z along the disc by the class's family device, so the term's non-minimiser defect is zero and (5.1c) applies at every t. The counterterm reads c(t) and its mismatch is bounded by (G.258), (|t|σ_c/α)(1 + C_P) times the counterterm's own bound, depth-small and marginal. The Cauchy estimate in t then gives (G.259)–(G.260), the product of age and depth plus n times the depth, absorbed by the LF rate's room. Option (a) of the r_z item follows at t = 1.

Not derived: the Lipschitz constant C_P of the local minimiser map (Lemma 4's analyticity with the norm identification unchecked), the gradient bound of 𝐒_c at the layer scale (assumed from the fine side's VAR norm), and (5.1c)'s own open inputs. Unreviewed.

Earlier attempts in this session, not written up: a family of layer-n minimisers of interpolated data fails at the endpoint, since the lift is a one-step minimiser; the one-step family U_1(□₀, 𝐕(t)) matches both endpoints but its members are not in 𝒮_z. Both are superseded by R-data, under which the lift enters only through its data.

## §50 (08:20–08:29): the U_{1,2} item

Hash at 08:29: note 4628 lines, 027765874ca9.

- Read LFR II p.370 ((1.50)–(1.54), the regularity remark on U_{1,2}) and the LF note's l.18, l.405–421, l.438, l.459–476.
- §50.2: U_{1,2} taken as a member (one configuration on the whole lattice). Every read of U_1 is a read of U_{1,2}, and U_2 is read only through it. Then (i) at U_{1,2} uses the same ι_X as every member, so no lift–patch commutator; r_z is the same expression; near domains at the cut go through §48.
- §50.3: the fine side at the cut. Size computation: the patch's cross-cut plaquettes are first order in the bond length, ≈ (ε/a_h)e^{−δ₀LMR_{h+1}}, against the second-order threshold α_{0,h}(ε/a_h)²; the ratio κ_cut carries a_h/ε = L^h, which the decay e^{−δ₀LMR_{h+1}} (R logarithmic in g) does not beat for h large. The same first-order sensitivity enters the joint line at U_{1,2} whichever lift is used (the side-lift patch gives the patched 𝐒 the same jump). Every Y_0 contains the cut in its component of Λ^∼, so it cannot be localised away. Flagged as an order-of-magnitude computation, not a refutation of Bałaban's p.370 statement, which gives κ_cut ≤ 1 only.

Caveat for s105 triage: the identification dι^L = H_1 d presumes VAR's Landau-gauge minimiser has PBG's double-bar constraint; VAR p.307 l.1-5 says only that the Landau and axial configurations are gauge equivalent. VAR p.305 l.37-46 and p.306 (179)-(180): the Landau gauge is fixed for U_1 = U_k(V'V_0)U_0^{-1}, and the configuration is H = A_0 + H_0 B - HD(A_0 + H_0 B), with Q~ or mixed averages allowed (p.306 l.24-25), and with G redefined as (Delta_a - Delta^(2))^{-1}. The B-linear part is therefore (1 - HD)(H_0 + dA_0); 10.5 must show that this equals PBG's H_1 d up to D_B gamma, or carry the -HD and dA_0 parts. Check this in the s105 triage. Partly checked: D(A') is the nonlinear part of the averaging, with |D(A')| <= 4C_2|A'|^2 (VAR p.285 l.46, p.286 l.24), so -HD contributes nothing at linear order at the base point V' = 1 (where the Landau configuration is 0). V is the functional of higher-order terms (VAR (80), p.290 l.24-31: -<HD_3,J>, <A',Delta_pi HD>, (1/2)<HD,Delta_pi HD>, and V_0(A'-HD)), so dV/dA' vanishes to first order at 0. By (143), dA_0 = G~ Delta^(2)(dA_0 + H_0 B), so H_0 + dA_0 = (1 - G~ Delta^(2))^{-1} H_0, the solution operator with Delta^(2) included; its equality with PBG's H_1 = G_1 Q*(Q G_1 Q*)^{-1}, G_1 of (3.128), is the resolvent algebra of (3.138) and not checked line by line. This is consistent with 10.5's identification (the averaging used, plain or double bar, as in the exactness paragraph). The shift is compared in the frozen units directly (smooth at the scale j). On m ≤ j_i the conversion to scale-m units costs c_N^{−1}, polynomial in g_{k_R}^{−2} since N₀ = O(log g_{k_R}^{−2}), and joins P(k). - 07:35, LF π–π term derived, not reviewed (LF sha 80eebe17b4b3; new sub-bullet *The π–π term.* in place of the Open sub-bullet; Open Gaps (6), the result-for-the-forms paragraph, and the E′ bullets updated). E₀^π = G₁^Qℒ*s_π with s_π the source of β_π(·, 𝒜). The earlier claim that this source concentrates was wrong: the composition gives each scale-m block the weight (a_m/a_j)⁴, and a bond's coefficient exceeds its block mean by at most L^{d−1}, so sup|s_π| ≤ c_s(1 + j)ε₁a_j^{−3}σ_k. The gradient entries telescope along the paths, which gives the factor j. Parameter-slot parts are T*n with sup bounds from the entries G′D* and DG′ (no τ̂). Then a|E₀^π| + a²|∇E₀^π| ≤ x_π(1 + j)ε₁σ_k, and the pairing route gives F_w^A(E₀^π, E₀^π) ≤ C_ππ(ε/a_n)^{1/3}ε₁²N_k. Points for review: the weights of the composition, the L^{d−1} path count, the transposed sup bounds, and the telescoping at the scales m ≥ 1 through Q^{(m)}D_A = D_{(m)}Q′^{(m)}.

- 3 Nov, from about 10:36, the interpolation lemma (I) of LF l.6 (working derivation, no referee):
  - Geometry, checked against the text:
    - PRT2 (2.3) (p.224) defines Λ_j both as a set of sites and as a set of bonds, the bonds with at least one endpoint in Λ_j. So the constraints Q_jA = B_j sit on the scale-j bonds inside the layer and on those crossing into Ω_{j+1}^{(j)}. The scale-(j+1) constraints include the bonds from Λ_{j+1} to the scale-(j+1) blocks of Ω_j ∖ Ω_{j+1}.
    - PBG p.408 l.20 and l.26: R and M are powers of L, and the big blocks of the scale j are the cells of the lattice ML^jηℤ^d. So every layer B^j(Λ_j) is a union of scale-(j+1) blocks, and its faces are flat over lengths ≥ ML^jη.
    - PBG (3.15) and (3.19) (p.393): Q_j and Q′_j are compositions of one-step operators. So the mean of α over a scale-(j+1) block inside B^j(Λ_j) is the average of the L^d scale-j means it contains.
    - Lattice units η = 1 below. In a layer of the scale i, S := L^i and S′ := L^{i+1}.
  - The data. From step (4) of LF l.6, m_i := Q′_iα on the whole lattice satisfies ∂_{(i)}m_i = Q_i∂α = Q_iD − Q_if, with f := ∂*β. Here Q_iD = 0 on the bonds of Λ_i, and also on the scale-(i+1) bonds of Ω_i ∖ Ω_{i+1}, because Q_{i+1} = Q∘Q_i and Bałaban's one-step contours for a bond stay in its two blocks. On Λ_0, m_0 = α and ∂α = −f, since D = 0 there.
  - The one-bond bound. For a scale-i bond b, the contours of Q_i are straight lines of length S starting at the S^d sites of a block. Each bond of Ω_b lies on at most S of them. So by Cauchy–Schwarz abs(Q_ig(b))² ≤ S^{2−d}‖g‖²_{Ω_b}. With ḡ the mean of f over a cube P of side ℓ, Q_iḡ is S times a component of ḡ. Summing gives Σ_{b⊂P} S^d abs(Q_i(f − ḡ)(b))² ≤ C(d)S²ℓ²‖∇f‖²_P by Poincaré on P.
  - The discrete Bramble–Hilbert bound. Let a be the affine function whose differences on P are the components of Sḡ (so a's block means are its values at the block centres). Discrete Poincaré on the N := ℓ/S blocks along each side of P, applied to m_i − a, gives S^dΣ_{B⊂P}abs(m_i − a)²_B ≤ C(d)N²S²ℓ²‖∇f‖²_P = C(d)ℓ⁴‖∇f‖²_P, uniformly in N. The constant in m_i − a is fixed by a's choice; only differences are used below.
  - Inside a layer. J_i(m) := Σ_B m_B N_B, with N_B the tensor-product quadratic B-spline on the cells of side S whose Greville point is the centre of B. It is a positive partition of unity of class C^{1,1}, and it reproduces affine functions, since the Greville points of quadratic B-splines are the cell midpoints. Its samples on the lattice have discrete second differences bounded by the sup of its continuous second derivatives. Hence, on a cell, ΔJ_i(m) = ΔJ_i(m − a) gives ‖ΔJ_i(m)‖²_cell ≤ C(d)S^{d−4}max_{3^d cells}abs(m − a)² ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖²_P for the cube P of side 3S around it. The means are then corrected: K_i := J_i(m) + Σ_B e_Bψ_B, e_B := m_B − mean_B J_i(m), with ψ_B a sampled smooth bump supported in B, of mean 1 and abs(Δψ_B) ≤ C(d)S^{−2} (S ≥ L ≥ 2). Since e_B vanishes on affine data, the same bound gives ‖Δ(e_Bψ_B)‖ ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖_P. Summing with bounded overlap gives ‖ΔK_i‖_{layer interior} ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖, uniformly in L.
  - At a flat part of an interface between the layers i and i + 1:
    - K_{i+1} is built in the same way from the scale-(i+1) means, using on the fine side the means of the scale-(i+1) blocks of B^i(Λ_i), which are averages of the m_i (the composition above). Its second-difference bound holds across the interface, because the coarse differences there are −Q_{i+1}f on bonds of Λ_{i+1}, or on scale-(i+1) bonds of Ω_i ∖ Ω_{i+1}, where Q_{i+1}D = 0.
    - On the fine side, d := m_i − (scale-i means of K_{i+1}). It has zero means over the scale-(i+1) blocks. The bounds above with ℓ = S′ give ‖G‖ ≤ C(d)S′²‖∇f‖, ‖∇G‖ ≤ C(d)S′‖∇f‖ and ‖∇²G‖ ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖ on a fine-side zone of width 3S′, for G := J_i(d) + Σ_B(d_B − mean_BJ_i(d))ψ_B. The term from the scale-i means of K_{i+1} is bounded by its own second derivatives, which are controlled at the scale S′.
    - The zone fits inside the layer, since the layers are ≥ RML^i wide (PRT2 (2.2)), with RM ≥ 3L.
    - G is extended across the face by the three-term Hestenes reflection, G(−t) := Σ_{k=1}^3 c_kG(kt) in the normal coordinate, with the c_k chosen so that quadratics are reproduced. The extension keeps the three bounds, with C(d), since each depth is used a bounded number of times and a dilated second difference at step k is a sum of k² unit ones.
    - It is cut off by χ, equal to 1 on the face and 0 at depth S′ in the coarse blocks, with abs(∇^kχ) ≤ C(d)S′^{−k}. Then ‖Δ(χEG)‖ ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖.
    - The coarse means are restored by one bump per coarse block B′ in the cut-off zone, of the size S′, with coefficient −mean_{B′}(χEG). This costs S′^{−4}S′^d·abs(mean)² ≤ S′^{−4}‖χEG‖²_{B′} ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖². The bound holds because the bump lives at the coarse width while G is of order S′².
    - I := K_{i+1} + G on the fine zone and K_{i+1} + χEG + coarse bumps on the coarse side. On the fine zone K_{i+1} + G has the fine means m_i.
    - Deeper in the fine layer, K_{i+1} + G is matched to K_i by a cutoff χ′ at the scale S′. Both have the fine means m_i, so the difference Φ := K_i − K_{i+1} − G has zero fine means. Up to the bump terms, Φ = J_i(scale-i means of K_{i+1}) − K_{i+1}. J_i reproduces affine functions, and K_{i+1} is smooth at the scale S′, so abs(Φ) ≤ C(d)S²abs(∇²K_{i+1}) and abs(∇Φ) ≤ C(d)S·abs(∇²K_{i+1}). The cutoff terms are then O((S/S′)²) and O(S/S′) times ∇²K_{i+1}. Since Φ has zero fine means, mean_B(χ′Φ) = mean_B((χ′ − χ′(c_B))Φ) = O((S/S′)S²∇²K_{i+1}), and the fine bumps restoring the means cost O(S/S′) times ∇²K_{i+1}. All are ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖.
  - Edges and corners of an interface:
    - If the fine side is locally one orthant or the complement of one, the reflections across the faces compose, or blend at the scale S′ with data that agree up to O(S′²) in the same norms, since the fine region around the edge is connected. No loss.
    - If big blocks of Λ_i and of Ω_{i+1} alternate around a codimension-2 face (the checkerboard pattern), each coarse block there borders both fine blocks. The two extensions are blended by an angular cutoff around the face. Their difference at distance r from it is the jump of the values plus r times the jump of the gradients plus O(r²∇²). The jumps are linked through the bonds of Λ_i into the fine sub-blocks of the coarse block that border both fine blocks, where ∂_{(i)} of the means is −Q_if. They are therefore bounded by the local fine differences of f. The cost is bounded dyadically, with no trace. Let U_r be the set of points within distance r of the face, inside one S′-patch, of volume about r²S′² in d = 4, for r = S, 2S, …, S′. On the shell U_{2r} ∖ U_r the angular cutoff has ∇² of order r^{−2}. The difference E_1 − E_2 there is r·γ_r + O(r²∇²), where γ_r, the jump of ∇d between the two sides, is a difference of f at points joined by paths of length O(r) around the face through the bonds of Λ_i. So γ_r² ≤ C(d)r²·avg_{U_{2r}}abs(∇f)², and the shell costs C(d)(rγ_r/r²)²r²S′² ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖²_{U_{2r}}. The O(r²∇²) part is bounded in the same way. The log₂L shells give ‖ΔI‖²_{near the face} ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)‖∇f‖²_{U_{S′}}. So ‖ΔI‖ ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^{1/2}‖∇f‖ at such faces.
    - This is harmless: LF l.6 needs C_H = o(L) in the factor (9/8)(1 + β₀)²(C_H + C(L)ε₁)/L, and the log appears only at blocks next to such faces.
  - The boundary of Ω_0. Λ_0 is the finest layer, with single-site blocks and I = α. Off Ω_0, I = α as well. Since D = 0 on Λ_0 and ∂*f = 0, Δα = ∂*D = 0 near ∂Ω_0. The Λ_0–Λ_1 interface is the case S = 1, S′ = L above.
  - Result (derived, not reviewed): (I) holds with ‖ΔI(Q′α)‖ ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^{1/2}‖∇f‖. The log factor comes only from checkerboard faces. Least detailed step: the trace bound on the checkerboard faces.

H_j, G̃_j and H′_j have constants depending on d only (Proposition 1.2, (2.130)–(2.132)). (2.147) inherits γ₀″ through (2.146).
  - The replacement. δ_{Ax(y)} enters only through the Faddeev–Popov identity (2.96), and the step after (2.103) uses only that the condition depends on Q_jA + ∂₁ω. So the block-local condition P_N∂₁*(Q_jA + ∂₁ω) = 0, P_N = 1 − L^dQ′₁*Q′₁, with a constant Faddeev–Popov determinant, gives (2.119)–(2.122) on the slice 𝒮, the L²-orthogonal complement of the coarse gauge directions. (A first version used R₁, the projection onto Δ₁N(Q′₁). It was replaced because R₁ is nonlocal, so its conjugation error is of first order in δ against a gap of order L^{−2}.) In (2.146), Q″*B ∈ 𝒮, so B₁ = Q″*B. ∂₁ω = 0 on the bonds of Λ^c, by p.241 after (2.103) and the bond convention of p.224. The Landau bound of LF l.6, with ‖∂ω‖ ≤ CL‖P_NΔω‖ for ω ∈ N, gives (2.122) ≥ c(1 + log L)^{−1}L^{−2} on Λ. Combes–Thomas for F_g = F + ‖P_N∂₁*B‖² gives the weighted-L² decay of C̃. Local regularity of Δ_j + ∂₁∂₁* plus terms of bounded rank per block gives the pointwise bounds. The random walk (2.141) converges for M ≥ L(1 + log L)^{p′}.

- 3 Nov, from about 11:15, triage of lf-iv-20261103-r36 (NOT ESTABLISHED, no fatal finding, 5 serious, clean count 0):

  - S9 (where L enters). l.6 now lists γ₀″, C′_Λ^{(j)} ((2.110), p.242 l.48), the normalisation ‖B₁‖² ≥ const‖B‖² behind the lower bound of (2.147) (p.248 l.42–48), (QGQ*)^{−1} ((3.132)) and (Q′G′²Q′*)^{−1} (PBG Theorem 3.2). This is open in the heading list.
  - S11/S19 (crossing bonds). The text checked: PRT2 (2.103) (p.241) is an "if and only if" for invariance: ∂₁μ = 0 on the Λ^c terms and ∂^LQ′₁μ = 0 on Λ′. Lemma 2.4 (p.245 l.77) takes Λ as the bonds with at least one endpoint in Λ, so the crossing bonds are in Λ there. PRT2 (2.3) puts the scale-j constraints on the crossing bonds of Λ_j. If the crossing bonds carry a Λ^c term, as the p.224 convention for the set Λ^c gives, then (2.103) forces μ = 0 also at the inner endpoints, which (2.104) does not state. Since the replacement of Lemma 2.4 makes its own gauge fixing, it uses the group N_b (ω = 0 on Λ^c and at the sites of Λ joined to Λ^c, mean zero on the blocks of Λ′). N_b is a group of invariances of the form under either convention, and the Faddeev–Popov identity needs only a linear slice of N_b, not an axial condition of matching dimension. P_N = 1 − Π_m − Π_∂ stays local on the blocks. In the multiscale form, the group is N_b(Q′) (Q′_jλ = 0 also at the scale-j sub-blocks y′ ⊂ Ω_{j+1}^{(j)} joined to Λ_j), under which the form is invariant with no cross terms, so S19's cross terms do not arise. (I) must then reproduce the means at the y′, which are data with ∂_{(j)}m_j = −Q_jf on the crossing bonds. The fine zone is Λ_i plus the layer of the y′, and the coarse mean bumps avoid the y′. r36's option of dropping the crossing terms was rejected. With it the two fine blocks at a checkerboard face are linked only through coarse bonds, and the value jump costs L².
  - S22 (value jump at checkerboard faces). Derivation (working, no referee):
    - Let the codimension-2 face have cross-section coordinates (x, y), with F₁ = {x, y > 0} and F₂ = {x, y < 0} fine, and C₁ = {x < 0 < y} and C₂ = {y < 0 < x} coarse.
    - The corner sub-block y′ of C₁ at (−S, 0)–(0, S) borders the corner fine blocks b₁ ⊂ F₁ (across x = 0) and b₂ ⊂ F₂ (across y = 0). With the crossing constraints, m(b₁) − m(y′) and m(y′) − m(b₂) are −Q_if on two scale-i bonds.
    - The deviations G_k (from F_k and its layer of y′) differ from m − (scale-i means of K_{i+1}) at their corner blocks by O(S)·abs(∇G). So the value jump at a face point z is J(z) = S·(f_S(z) − f_{S′}(z))·O(1) + O(S)·(gradient jump). Here f_r(z) is the average of f over the ball of radius r about z, and the scale-i means of K_{i+1} carry the differences of K_{i+1}, whose gradient is f at the scale S′.
    - The blend costs (J/r²)²·r² per unit face area on the shell of radius r, so Σ_r J²/r² ≤ C(d)J²/S², and with the fine bumps that restore the means of the corner sub-blocks within 3S of the face, at the same order.
    - With the dyadic radii r_k = 2^kS, k < log₂L, abs(f_S − f_{S′})² ≤ log₂L·Σ_k abs(f_{r_k} − f_{2r_k})², and Poincaré on B(z, 2r_k) gives abs(f_{r_k}(z) − f_{2r_k}(z))² ≤ C(d)r_k^{2−d}‖∇f‖²_{B(z,2r_k)}. Integrated over the face points z of one S′-patch, ∫dz r_k^{2−d}‖∇f‖²_{B(z,2r_k)} ≤ C(d)‖∇f‖²_{U_{2r_k}}.
    - So the cost is ≤ C(d)log L·Σ_k‖∇f‖²_{U_{2r_k}} ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)²‖∇f‖²_{U_{2S′}}, since each shell lies in at most log₂L of the U_{2r_k}.
    - Hence (I) holds with ‖ΔI(Q′α)‖ ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)‖∇f‖. The exponents in l.6 changed accordingly: the Landau bound has the factor (1 + log L), F ≥ c(1 + log L)^{−2}(…) on 𝒮, and the decay rate is c(1 + log L)^{−1} in units of L.
  - S13 (Combes–Thomas). The reviewer's "dual identity" (1 − P_N)∂₁*B = L^dQ′₁*∂^{L*}Q₁B was checked in d = 1 and is false in general: the block mean of ∂₁*B is the flux through the faces, while Q₁B averages along contours of length L. The control used instead is: for ω ∈ N_b, ‖Π_mΔω‖ ≤ C(d)‖P_NΔω‖. Proof with the test function t = L²Σ_yc_yχ_y, χ_y vanishing near the faces of y, so t = 0 on the ∂_y: c(d)L²‖v‖² ≤ ⟨∇t, ∇ω⟩ − ⟨P_Nt, P_NΔω⟩ ≤ C(d)L²‖v‖‖P_NΔω‖, using ‖∇t‖ ≤ CL‖v‖, ‖P_Nt‖ ≤ CL²‖v‖ and ‖∇ω‖ ≤ CL‖P_NΔω‖. A first attempt through ⟨v, Δ^{−1}v⟩ lost L^{1/2} at the blocks next to Λ^c, through the Π_∂ term. For general B, ‖Π_m∂₁*B‖ ≤ ‖∂₁*B_L‖ + C‖P_N∂₁*B‖ ≤ C(1 + log L)F_g(B)^{1/2}. Π_∂ commutes with the weight, and [Π_m, η] is O(δ) on P_Nu and on Π_mu. So the Combes–Thomas errors are relative O(δ(1 + log L)).
  - S14. ∂₁Π_m∂₁*u is in divergence form, and its block constants are bounded in L² by the bound above. The Π_∂ and Λ^c terms are local boundary terms on the flat faces of Λ. The pointwise bound at the blocks next to Λ^c is listed open.

So the exterior data are c's own values. (a) F(D) := 𝓗_old(M_old(𝓗_new(D))) − 𝓗_new(D) is analytic on the convex polydisc of c's data, and it vanishes at the real points, by the real argument. So F ≡ 0.

The factor per level uses only the sup of Im c in field units, bounded relative to the thresholds of the scale i_prev of c's own data (the step k for the literal points, whose complex variables are of the scales ≥ k, Cauchy l.148), and the reading scale i < i_prev of the old term. Cauchy l.148 gives (1+β₀)²(i_prev − i)^{1/2}L^{−(i_prev−i)} ≤ (9/8)(1+β₀)²L^{−1}. c's own finer blocks do not enter, since the old member reads c's scale-i averages, whose complex parts are ≤ L^iη sup abs(Im c). So alternating chains are covered.

The rest is T = ∂₁*∂₁ + ∂₁(1 − 1_∂)∂₁* + 1_{Λ^c}: L-independent, Δ on the bonds of Λ at distance ≥ 1 from ∂, massive on Λ^c. What remains is abs(G_T) ≤ C(d)abs(x − y)^{2−d} and abs(∇G_T) ≤ C(d)abs(x − y)^{1−d} for them. For the half-space, eliminating Λ^c and ∂ at tangential momentum k gives Δ on s ≥ 1 with a positive semidefinite DtN boundary matrix, so ‖r(k)‖ ≤ 1. Not yet shown: the tangential decay of the reflected term (smoothness of r in k near k = 0), and the corner operators.

- 3 Nov, about 12:15–12:25, triage of lf-iv-20261103-r37 (NOT ESTABLISHED, no fatal finding, 4 serious, 10 minor, clean count 0). Before it landed, the author found that the stated open sub-step is false at the re-entrant edges and corners of Λ. The repairs are written into LF l.6 (now ba74b71842b9, 996 lines); table b821b83673f7 (l.3 pin, row 226); the fork was told before and after.
  - Corner finding (author). PRT2 (2.97) (p.240 l.26) puts a unit-scale mass on the bonds with an endpoint in Λ^c, while Λ carries only aL^{d−2}abs(Q_{j+1}A)². ∂₁*u is not penalised at the sites ∂, so the normal component is free at the interface: on the fibre of tangential momentum 0, u = 1 on the normal bonds of Λ and 0 on the crossing bonds has energy → 0 under a cutoff at large distance. The scaling limit therefore has the relative boundary condition. At the re-entrant edges and corners the slice-gauge response carries gradients of the Dirichlet singular functions, of size (L(1 + log L))^{1−ν}, ν = π/ω (2/3 at ω = 3π/2), smaller at the vertices. So abs(∇G_T) ≤ C abs(x − y)^{1−d}, and even the field bound in the slice gauge, fail by a power of L. An intermediate note in the session claiming componentwise Dirichlet behaviour was wrong for this reason. Units: RG I p.257 l.3 gives ξ = L^{−j}, so (1.13)–(1.14) compare physical gradients and plaquettes with the field thresholds, and no 1/L gain of the gradient is needed.
  - S3 (Δ_j → Δ as a source is not a perturbation). Accepted. Repair: Δ_j = ∂₁*K∂₁ is gauge-invariant, so applying ∂₁ removes the gauge-fixing term. With au = g′ − ∂₁*KF on the massive bonds, the curvature solves (∂₁1_Λ∂₁*K + ∂₁*∂₁)F = ∂₁(1_Λg′), ∂₁F = 0, which is gauge-invariant with no layer term. The members are read at the global Landau representative v = ∂₁*Δ^{−1}F, which removes the pure-gauge corner parts. sup abs(v) is bounded by the Riesz potential of F, which is a power of 1 + log L times the bulk under a Morrey bound for F with any γ > 0. Open: that lattice Morrey bound at the faces, edges, and vertices (heading (a)). The continuum exponent at an edge is π/ω; the vertex exponents are not checked.
  - S1 (C′_Λ and the R-operators enter Bałaban's H, and B₀ through (2.129)). Accepted for the old text. Repair: C_H is taken for v, whose response is the transverse part of the response in any gauge, so neither the slice nor R enters it. B₀ enters VAR only through B₃, B₅ ((162), (173)) and the ε₁-correction of (187) (r37 §1.2), so Bałaban's L-dependent constants for B₀ are admissible, and the random walk (2.141) is used only for B₀.
  - S4 (the G^c change of representative is unbounded pointwise). Accepted, together with the fork's TB4-1 correction (RG I (1.19) holds for 𝐄^{(j)} only). Repair: 𝐄^{(j)} by (1.19) on the orbit-saturated U_j^c; the other invariant members by the identity principle (the two-loops note's l.130 (b)). Covariant members: open, heading (b), the same issue as the two-loops note's Open (R-eval-curv).
  - S2 ("the gauge part separates" for N(Q′)). Accepted; replaced by the reviewer's lower bound through N_b(Q′) and R_b, ‖R∂*A‖ ≥ ‖R_b∂*A‖ = ‖Δλ‖; step (3) now reads ‖(1 − R_b)∂*D‖ = min, ≥ ‖(1 − R)∂*D‖ (M7).
  - Minor. M1: P_N on ℓ²(ℤ^d) is 1 − Π_m − Π_∂ − 1_{Λ^c}, written. M2: the vacuous decomposition on 𝒮 is replaced by the Landau bound on 𝒮 and the Poincaré bound for ω ∈ N_b. M3: the exterior data are cited to CRE p.256 l.57–63, (2.14), and RG I (1.15), checked at p.256–257. M4: the strictness i < i_prev is derived from (α). M5: U₀ is not stated real; the product term is of order C(L, M)ε₁θ, part of the background term; the contour length is 2dL^kη. M6: the L² cost without the crossing constraints is an estimate for one construction, not a lower bound; it is not used.
